## 2. Planning conclusion

Production-readiness sign-off is not supported by the locked audit. The final audit has no PASS or NOT APPLICABLE controls, includes 117 unresolved P0 controls, and records 29 confirmed P0 failures. The dominant blockers are incomplete production configuration and deployment evidence, unresolved identity and authorization policy, missing durable background processing and reconciliation, incomplete current critical-workflow tests, absent observability and operational processes, and unverified provider, backup, restore, and recovery behavior.

This plan converts the locked findings into one dependency-aware program. It assigns every unresolved control exactly one primary workstream, identifies secondary dependencies, separates implementation from decisions and evidence collection, and defines objective entry and exit gates.

The plan does not implement fixes, select unresolved policy values, verify providers, execute tests, or close any control. Implementation must be phased because foundational decisions, deployment topology, database invariants, and durable processing must stabilize before dependent payment, storage, browser, observability, and recovery evidence can be trusted.

## 3. Remediation classification model

| Remediation type | Meaning |
|---|---|
| CODE | Application source changes to routes, services, schemas, clients, middleware, workers, or related logic. |
| DATABASE | PostgreSQL schema, constraints, indexes, roles, transaction behavior, and durable state. |
| MIGRATION | Alembic graph, expand-and-contract changes, backfills, compatibility, interruption, and rehearsal. |
| STATIC CONFIGURATION | Versioned application, build, test, security, or environment configuration. |
| DEPLOYMENT | Runtime process, artifact, edge, health gate, rollout, shutdown, and platform configuration. |
| CURRENT TESTS | Current non-legacy unit, service, API, PostgreSQL, browser, provider, migration, concurrency, and failure tests. |
| CI / SUPPLY CHAIN | CI gates, tool and dependency reproducibility, scans, artifacts, repository protections, SBOM, and provenance. |
| OWNER DECISION | Approved decision record where no universal setting or policy is already locked. |
| PROVIDER EVIDENCE | Redacted settings and behavior from Vercel, API hosting, DNS/CDN, Firebase, Stripe, R2, PostgreSQL, GitHub, monitoring, or backup control planes. |
| RUNTIME VERIFICATION | Observed behavior in a safe staging or production-equivalent environment using synthetic or sanitized data. |
| OPERATIONAL PROCESS | Owned procedures, inventories, reviews, approvals, runbooks, audit records, and recurring operations. |
| OBSERVABILITY | Structured logs, correlation, metrics, service objectives, dashboards, alerts, capacity, and loss detection. |
| RECOVERY / EXERCISE | Backup, restore, failover, replay, tabletop, incident, and technical recovery evidence. |
| PRIVACY / RETENTION | Data classification, lifecycle, access, correction, export, deletion, anonymization, retention, and backup/restore handling. |

One control may require several remediation types. A repository change alone does not close a control when the locked finding also requires current tests, provider settings, runtime behavior, operational approval, or recovery evidence.

Parallelization is allowed only where the listed prerequisites are satisfied. Phase 1A and the secret/control-plane subset of Phase 1B can proceed together. WS03 and WS04 can proceed in parallel after their policy and topology inputs are stable. WS05 and WS06 may split after the shared database and durable-job foundation is defined. WS08 and WS09 begin as enabling tracks but cannot reach their final gates until the implementation they verify is stable.

**Sequencing clarification:** WS08 and WS09 have early enabling slices, not completed-workstream prerequisites. WS08 establishes taxonomy, fixtures, and harness rules during Phase 1; its final CI and release gates occur after stable implementation. WS09 establishes correlation, event-envelope, privacy, and telemetry contracts during Phase 1; its production dashboards, alerts, and capacity gates occur later. WS04 receives approved invariant and durable-state requirements from WS05 and WS06 as design inputs, while WS05 and WS06 still depend on the completed WS04 foundation for implementation. Within WS05, the shared job lifecycle is implemented before payment, notice, moderation, and reconciliation consumers.

## 6. Detailed workstream plans

### WS01: Governance decisions and production ownership

#### A. Objective

Establish the authoritative decisions, ownership, architecture records, audit cadence, and exception process that govern every later technical and operational change.

#### B. Locked findings addressed

| Control ID | Priority | Final status | Locked gap | Why included |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| GOV-001 | P0 | PARTIAL | No authoritative architecture inventory covering workers, scheduler, monitoring, backups, DNS/CDN, regions, public and private services, or single points of failure. Search scope is detailed in GOV-001 appendix. | It establishes the governing decision, ownership, audit, or exception baseline. |
| GOV-004 | P1 | NEEDS DECISION | No named production owners for authentication, authorization, payments, database, storage, workers, release, secrets, monitoring, backups, incident response, privacy operations, or provider accounts. Search scope is detailed in GOV-004 appendix. | It establishes the governing decision, ownership, audit, or exception baseline. |
| GOV-005 | P1 | FAIL | No cadence, approval, versioning, exception handling, preserved production-readiness results, reassessment triggers, or stable source-revision linkage. This one-time static audit and prior generated reports are not repository evidence. | It establishes the governing decision, ownership, audit, or exception baseline. |
| GOV-006 | P1 | NEEDS DECISION | No documented basis and boundary-test evidence for universal request size, timeout, rate, worker count, pool size, indexes, retries, retention, RPO, RTO, or alert thresholds. Search scope is detailed in GOV-006 appendix. | It establishes the governing decision, ownership, audit, or exception baseline. |
| GOV-007 | P1 | FAIL | No repository-verifiable risk acceptance or exception-management process. | It establishes the governing decision, ownership, audit, or exception baseline. |

#### C. Required decisions

| Decision ID or control | Decision required | Options or dimensions to evaluate | Dependencies | Suggested owner role | Decision evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| API-M18 | Decide OpenAPI/docs exposure, inventory, versioning, compatibility, and deprecation policy. | Choose and verify OpenAPI/docs exposure, maintain endpoint inventory, and manage compatibility, deprecation, and versioning deliberately. Hiding documentation must not substitute for authorization. | WS08, WS01 | API owner | Approved API docs/version policy plus runtime exposure evidence. |
| TST-011 | Decide retry, flake, artifact retention, and risk-based coverage policy. | Treat retries as diagnostic aids, not defect masking. Track flaky tests with owner and expiry; retain sanitized artifacts and root-cause recurring failures. Use risk-based coverage rather than a universal percentage. | WS01 | QA or engineering owner | Approved test policy plus CI artifact evidence. |
| GOV-004 | Assign named production owners across core systems and provider accounts. | Assign named owners for authentication, authorization, payments, database, storage, workers, deployment, secrets, monitoring, backups, incident response, privacy operations, and provider accounts. | WS02, WS03, WS04, WS05, WS06, WS07, WS08, WS09, WS10 | Engineering leadership | Owner matrix plus provider-access records. |
| GOV-006 | Decide documented bases for limits, thresholds, pools, retries, retention, RPO, RTO, and alerts. | Do not invent universal limits for request size, timeout, rate, worker count, pool size, indexes, retries, retention, RPO, RTO, or alert thresholds. Each value must have a documented basis and boundary test. | WS02, WS04, WS05, WS06, WS09, WS10 | Engineering, product, and platform owners | Approved limit basis plus boundary-test evidence. |

#### D. Repository implementation scope

| Component or area | Planned change category | Intended outcome | Related controls | Dependency | Risk if sequenced incorrectly |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Governance documentation | OPERATIONAL PROCESS | Create authoritative architecture, ownership, decision, and exception records. | GOV-001, GOV-004, GOV-006, GOV-007 | Validated audit and stakeholder participation | Technical work proceeds without accountable decisions or traceability. |
| Production-readiness audit records | OPERATIONAL PROCESS | Create a repeatable evidence, status, approval, exception, and reassessment workflow. | GOV-005 | Stable control manifest and owner register | Future audits become one-time snapshots with no source linkage or follow-through. |
| Decision registry | OWNER DECISION | Record unresolved thresholds and domain policies without inventing values. | GOV-006 and all MUST DECIDE controls | Named decision owners and required participants | Teams embed conflicting assumptions in code and operations. |
| Risk and exception registry | OPERATIONAL PROCESS | Require owner, rationale, compensating control, expiry/review date, and verification evidence. | GOV-007 | Ownership and audit cadence | P0 gaps are informally accepted without time-bound accountability. |
| Architecture and trust boundaries | STATIC CONFIGURATION / OPERATIONAL PROCESS | Maintain system, provider, data-flow, region, public/private boundary, and single-point-of-failure records. | GOV-001 | Provider and deployment inventory | Later security, recovery, and capacity work is based on an incomplete topology. |

#### E. Database and migration implications

No direct production schema change is required merely to create governance records. If the project later stores approvals, exceptions, or evidence inside the application, that design requires separate access control, append-only history, retention, and migration review. Governance artifacts should not be coupled to a speculative application schema during this planning phase.

#### F. Current-test plan

| Test layer | Required coverage | Failure cases | Concurrency or replay needs | Related controls | Completion evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Document validation | Required fields and cross-reference checks for owner, decision, exception, approval, expiry, and control IDs. | Missing owner, stale review date, invalid control ID, unresolved dependency. | Not applicable. | GOV-001, GOV-004 through GOV-007 | Automated or reviewed validation report with zero missing required fields. |
| Audit-process rehearsal | Run the audit workflow against a controlled sample change. | Missing source linkage, skipped approval, stale exception, unpreserved evidence. | Reassessment trigger replay. | GOV-005, GOV-007 | Preserved sample audit package and approval trail. |

#### G. Runtime and provider verification

| Verification target | Environment | Safe evidence | Related controls | Prerequisite |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Architecture accuracy | Production/provider inventory review | Approved diagram and inventory with provider settings referenced safely. | GOV-001 | Provider owners available. |
| Ownership coverage | Cross-functional review | Signed ownership matrix and escalation contacts. | GOV-004 | All control-plane owners identified. |
| Decision closure | Governance review | Decision records showing status, options considered, rationale, participants, and downstream controls. | GOV-006 and decision register | Decision owners assigned. |

#### H. Operational deliverables

| Deliverable | Purpose | Suggested owner role | Related controls | Approval or exercise evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Production architecture and trust-boundary inventory | Authoritative system and dependency baseline. | Platform architecture owner | GOV-001 | Engineering and security approval. |
| Production ownership register | Assign accountable owners and recovery contacts. | Engineering leadership | GOV-004 | Leadership approval and provider-access cross-check. |
| Production-readiness audit process | Define cadence, evidence, approval, source linkage, exceptions, and reassessment. | Release governance owner | GOV-005 | Completed sample audit cycle. |
| Limits and thresholds decision record | Capture rationale and validation method without inventing values. | Engineering and operations leadership | GOV-006 | Approved decisions and linked boundary tests. |
| Risk and exception register | Govern accepted risk with compensating controls and expiry. | Security/risk owner | GOV-007 | Periodic review and expired-exception test. |

#### I. Entry criteria

- Finalized Parts 1 through 6 are locked and available.
- A decision owner and required participants can be named for each governance artifact.

#### J. Exit criteria

- Every governance control has an approved artifact with stable control-ID linkage.
- All downstream workstreams know which decisions are final, open, or explicitly deferred.
- No P0 gap is treated as accepted without a time-bound exception record.

#### K. Risks and rollback considerations

- Approving vague owner groups instead of named accountable roles.
- Embedding unapproved numeric thresholds into technical plans.
- Treating the remediation plan itself as the repeatable audit process.
- Allowing exceptions without expiry, compensating controls, or re-verification.

### WS02: Production configuration and deployment foundation

#### A. Objective

Create a typed, environment-isolated, securely deployed HTTP runtime with deliberate process, proxy, TLS, CORS, limits, timeout, caching, health, compatibility, and artifact behavior.

#### B. Locked findings addressed

| Control ID | Priority | Final status | Locked gap | Why included |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| GOV-002 | P0 | PARTIAL | No complete environment matrix for local, automated test, browser test, provider sandbox, staging, and production across Firebase, Stripe, R2, PostgreSQL, domains, webhooks, secrets, logs, and CI credentials. | It governs production configuration, HTTP behavior, deployment, or release foundations. |
| API-M01 | P1 | PARTIAL | Lifecycle search over backend/main.py, backend/database.py, and backend/services/* for lifespan, on_event, startup, shutdown, and dispose found no FastAPI lifecycle ownership or graceful teardown of shared resources. | It governs production configuration, HTTP behavior, deployment, or release foundations. |

#### D. Repository implementation scope

| Component or area | Planned change category | Intended outcome | Related controls | Dependency | Risk if sequenced incorrectly |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Backend settings and startup | CODE / STATIC CONFIGURATION | Typed production validation, controlled lifecycle, readiness semantics, and safe teardown. | GOV-002, API-M01, API-M02, API-M17 | WS01 environment and ownership decisions | Import-time behavior or unsafe defaults survive into production. |
| Process and deployment configuration | DEPLOYMENT | Deliberate runtime command, supervision, concurrency, shutdown, health gates, and artifact identity. | API-M03, OPS-001 through OPS-004 | Connection budget and deployment topology | Process count or rolling overlap exceeds database/provider limits. |
| Proxy, host, TLS, CORS, and headers | STATIC CONFIGURATION / DEPLOYMENT | Assign trusted layers and enforce exact edge/origin behavior. | API-M04 through API-M08 | Provider topology and browser policy decisions | Direct-origin bypass, spoofed headers, redirect loops, or cross-origin overexposure. |
| Limits, timeouts, rate controls, and errors | CODE / STATIC CONFIGURATION | Apply bounded work before expensive operations and stable failure semantics. | API-M09 through API-M12 | GOV-006 decisions and WS09 correlation conventions | Inconsistent layered limits or retries create denial-of-service or duplicate effects. |
| HTTP contracts and schemas | CODE | Deliberate methods, media types, request/response separation, pagination, and compatibility. | API-M13, API-M14, API-M18 | API ownership and versioning decision | Breaking changes or over-broad schemas spread to frontend and clients. |
| Cache, health, and end-to-end chain | CODE / DEPLOYMENT / CURRENT TESTS | Protect private responses, expose minimal health, and verify full HTTP chain. | API-M16, API-M17, API-M19 | Stable edge and provider configuration | Private data is cached or configured checks are mistaken for runtime proof. |

#### E. Database and migration implications

Process count, pool budget, request timeouts, and readiness behavior must be designed with WS04. Settings changes must not silently alter connection usage. Any health query must remain minimal and must not become a migration or privileged diagnostic path. Rolling deployment compatibility must be considered before changing API schemas or response semantics.

#### F. Current-test plan

| Test layer | Required coverage | Failure cases | Concurrency or replay needs | Related controls | Completion evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Unit/config tests | Typed settings, unsafe default rejection, host/CORS/header/error/cache decisions. | Missing values, malformed origins, direct-origin bypass, unsupported media type, private cache response. | Not applicable. | GOV-002, API-M02, API-M05 through API-M08, API-M12, API-M16 | Current tests prove invalid production configuration fails before readiness. |
| API integration tests | Methods, media types, error contract, limits, pagination, health, and cache behavior. | Malformed JSON, oversized requests, timeout, rate limit, unsupported media, stale version. | Parallel limit/rate cases where relevant. | API-M09 through API-M14, API-M16 through API-M19 | Current non-legacy API tests with stable response assertions. |
| Deployment tests | Startup, readiness, shutdown, rolling compatibility, and proxy behavior. | Failed startup, SIGTERM during request, bad forwarded headers, redirect loop, unhealthy instance. | Rolling overlap and concurrent requests. | API-M01, API-M03, API-M04, API-M06, API-M17, OPS-001 through OPS-004 | Staging evidence and automated smoke results tied to release artifact. |
| Security chain tests | Host spoofing, forwarded-header spoofing, ambiguous framing, CORS and cache boundaries. | Direct origin, untrusted proxy, disallowed origin, conflicting framing, cache replay. | Multi-hop proxy cases. | API-M04, API-M05, API-M07, API-M16, API-M19 | Authorized end-to-end test report. |

| Verification target | Environment | Safe evidence | Related controls | Prerequisite |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Frontend and API environment isolation | Local, CI, provider sandbox, staging, production | Redacted environment matrix and provider-project identifiers. | GOV-002 | WS01 owner approval. |
| Edge and origin controls | Staging production-equivalent edge | Header captures, redirect traces, trusted-proxy observations, direct-origin denial evidence. | API-M04 through API-M08 | Provider configuration applied. |
| Runtime lifecycle | Staging deployment | Startup/shutdown logs, readiness transitions, connection release observations. | API-M01, API-M03, API-M17, OPS-001, OPS-004 | Deployable artifact and health gates. |
| Limits and timeouts | Staging with safe synthetic load | Boundary results, 413/429/timeout responses, resource release and correlation evidence. | API-M09 through API-M12 | Approved limits and telemetry. |

#### H. Operational deliverables

| Deliverable | Purpose | Suggested owner role | Related controls | Approval or exercise evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Environment matrix | Prevent cross-environment provider and credential mixing. | Platform owner | GOV-002 | Owner review and provider cross-check. |
| Deployment topology and process record | Document runtime command, instances, workers, rolling overlap, health, and shutdown. | Platform owner | API-M03, OPS-001 | Staging deployment observation. |
| Edge ownership matrix | Assign proxy, host, TLS, HSTS, CORS, header, and cache responsibilities. | Platform/security owner | API-M04 through API-M08, API-M16 | Provider screenshots and response verification. |
| HTTP compatibility policy | Define API inventory, docs exposure, versioning, and deprecation. | API owner | API-M18, API-M19 | Approved policy and compatibility tests. |
| Release and rollback record | Preserve artifact, health gate, rollback/forward-fix, and compatibility evidence. | Release owner | OPS-004 | Release rehearsal. |

#### I. Entry criteria

- WS01 assigns platform, API, security, and release owners.
- Environment and deployment topology decisions are available.
- Connection and timeout decisions are coordinated with WS04 and WS09.

#### J. Exit criteria

- Production configuration fails closed on missing or unsafe values.
- Staging proves proxy, TLS, CORS, host, limits, timeout, error, cache, health, startup, and shutdown behavior.
- The deployed artifact and provider settings are attributable and reviewable.
- Current tests cover the HTTP chain without counting legacy tests.

#### K. Risks and rollback considerations

- Changing process count without an approved database connection budget.
- Duplicating TLS/header ownership at edge and app and creating loops or conflicting headers.
- Applying retries to non-idempotent operations.
- Breaking frontend behavior through schema or cache changes without compatibility coverage.

### WS03: Identity, account state, authorization, and admin security

#### A. Objective

Make identity authority, account state, object/function/field authorization, high-risk action controls, moderation access, and minimum-necessary admin data explicit and testable.

| Provisioning and lifecycle | CODE / DATABASE | Make first login, account disablement, deletion, recovery, and cross-instance state transitions conflict-safe. | IAM-005, IAM-009, IAM-018 | WS04 uniqueness and transaction design | Duplicate users or stale access during lifecycle changes. |
| High-risk authentication and provider controls | OWNER DECISION / PROVIDER EVIDENCE | Define recent auth, step-up, MFA, App Check applicability, service-account scope, and emergency revocation. | IAM-008, IAM-010, IAM-011 | Provider capabilities and risk decisions | Sensitive actions remain protected only by a broad active session. |
| Authorization matrix | CODE / CURRENT TESTS | Enforce and prove route, object, relationship, workflow-state, list, field, function, and admin action authorization. | IAM-012 through IAM-017 | Stable resource/state model and DB invariants | IDOR, mass assignment, stale-role, or over-broad admin access persists. |
| Moderation taxonomy and review states | CODE / DATABASE | Version findings, bind evidence, manage review cases, enforcement, and notice policy. | ADM-009, ADM-010, ADM-012 through ADM-014 | WS04 transaction model and WS05 delivery path | Stale evidence, conflicting actions, or unsafe notices. |
| Minimum-necessary admin data | CODE / OPERATIONAL PROCESS | Use excerpt-first responses, controlled unmasking, anti-caching, and read auditing. | ADM-007, ADM-015 | WS09 audit/correlation design | Private messages and sensitive financial context remain broadly exposed. |

#### E. Database and migration implications

Likely implications include protecting verifier-controlled fields, enforcing identity uniqueness and state transitions, adding action-specific permission data only if the approved authorization design requires it, and supporting immutable moderation/enforcement history. Changes must preserve existing identifiers and avoid locking users out during rolling deployment. Backfills require explicit source-of-truth rules and conflict handling.

| API authorization matrix | Route, object, nested resource, relationship, state, list/search, field, function, admin action. | Cross-user ID substitution, stale role, privilege removal, hidden resource, mass assignment. | Simultaneous privilege/state changes where relevant. | IAM-012 through IAM-017, ADM-007, ADM-013, ADM-015, TST-005 | Complete negative matrix with expected 403/404 and no data leakage. |
| Moderation tests | Finding version/hash, stale detection, case transitions, concurrent review, enforcement idempotency, notices. | Edit after finding, duplicate scan, conflict, reversal, notice suppression, private evidence access. | Concurrent review and duplicate enforcement. | ADM-009, ADM-010, ADM-012 through ADM-015 | Current PostgreSQL-backed tests and audit assertions. |
| Browser identity tests | Persistence, logout, user switch, suspension, deletion, admin privilege removal. | Open-tab stale state, browser history, cached private data, direct route. | Cross-tab or sequential identity changes. | IAM-003, IAM-004, IAM-009, FE-M05, FE-M06, FE-M10 | Current Playwright results against production build. |

#### H. Operational deliverables

| Deliverable | Purpose | Suggested owner role | Related controls | Approval or exercise evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Identity source-of-truth matrix | Define authoritative fields and synchronization/conflict behavior. | Auth/data owner | IAM-006, IAM-007 | Security and data approval. |
| High-risk action policy | Define actions requiring recent auth, MFA, dual control, or compensating controls. | Security owner | IAM-008, IAM-017 | Threat/risk review and staged evidence. |

#### I. Entry criteria

- Identity, security, trust-and-safety, and privacy owners are assigned.
- IAM-003, IAM-006, IAM-007, IAM-010, and ADM-014 decisions are recorded where required.
- IAM-008 remains a required high-risk-authentication implementation and evidence control; it is not added as a twenty-eighth owner-decision-register entry.
- WS02 provides isolated Firebase/staging environments.
- WS04 can support required uniqueness and state constraints.

#### J. Exit criteria

- Ordinary users cannot mutate verifier-controlled fields.
- Account state and role changes deny access across routes and open sessions as designed.
- Authorization matrices are complete and current tests cover negative substitutions and field/function boundaries.
- Sensitive admin data is minimum-necessary and controlled unmask/read access is auditable.
- Provider and runtime evidence matches approved policies.

#### K. Risks and rollback considerations

- Locking out valid users through incorrect authority/backfill rules.
- Relying on frontend guards instead of backend authorization.
- Exposing existence through inconsistent 403/404 behavior.
- Introducing broad permission tables without clear action semantics.
- Changing moderation evidence or notice behavior without preserving historical records.

### WS04: Database lifecycle, transactions, concurrency, and migrations

#### A. Objective

Define safe engine/session lifecycle, transaction and external-side-effect boundaries, database invariants, concurrency policy, least-privilege roles, and rolling-safe migrations.

#### E. Database and migration implications

This workstream owns the database and migration design. Schema changes must use forward-compatible phases, explicit constraints/indexes, and durable workflow states. Backfills must be resumable and observable when production volume makes one transaction unsafe. Migration review must address lock duration, old/new code compatibility, failure recovery, and provider role separation before deployment.

| Deterministic concurrency tests | Capacity, one-active-record, webhook, credits/refunds, admin actions, job claims. | Deadlock, lock timeout, serialization failure, unknown commit, loser outcome. | Barrier-coordinated winner/loser assertions. | DB-007, DB-008, TST-008 | Repeatable concurrency suite with stable expected outcomes. |
| Migration tests | Empty and prior production-like upgrades, drift, multi-head, interruption, old/new overlap. | Blocking DDL, failed backfill, restart, rollback/forward-fix, data loss. | Concurrent old/new app access during compatibility window. | DB-016 through DB-018, TST-009 | Migration reports and preserved fixtures/artifacts. |
| Role and privilege tests | Application, migration, worker, read-only, backup role boundaries. | Owner/superuser use, unsafe search path, unexpected DDL/DML access. | Not applicable. | DB-015 | Redacted grant verification and denied-operation tests. |

#### I. Entry criteria

- WS02 process topology is known.
- DB-002 connection budget and DB-011 lifecycle decision are assigned or approved as applicable.
- WS05/WS06 provide required durable state and invariant definitions.
- A current PostgreSQL integration environment is available.

#### J. Exit criteria

- Every critical workflow has explicit transaction and external-side-effect boundaries.
- Database constraints or deliberate serialization protect concurrent invariants.
- Current deterministic concurrency tests cover winner, loser, retry, and unknown-outcome cases.
- Roles and grants are least-privilege and evidenced.
- Migrations pass empty and production-like rehearsal with old/new compatibility and recovery evidence.

#### K. Risks and rollback considerations

- Adding constraints before cleaning or backfilling incompatible data.
- Holding transactions open across provider calls.
- Retrying only fragments of a transaction after serialization failure.
- Adding indexes or constraints with blocking operations on production-sized tables.
- Changing pool settings without total deployment budget.

| Moderation and notice durable work | CODE / DATABASE | Represent scanner failure and large-audience delivery as durable, observable work. | ADM-011, ADM-016 | Job foundation and WS03 policies | Timeouts or partial delivery appear clean and cannot be resumed. |

#### E. Database and migration implications

Likely schema work includes a general durable job table or equivalent, attempt/lease/error/result state, financial reconciliation and dispute state where approved, delivery attempts, and durable provider-operation records. Designs must support idempotent claims, unique provider event identities, append-only ledgers, and old/new worker compatibility. Backfills must avoid synthesizing provider truth without reconciliation.

| Failure-injection tests | Worker crash, provider timeout, DB commit uncertainty, retry exhaustion, partial notice/moderation work. | Crash before/after side effect, lost acknowledgment, unavailable provider, failed evidence write. | Replay and multi-worker recovery. | JOB-M03 through JOB-M08, ADM-011, ADM-016 | Reproducible failure-injection report and repair outcome. |

#### I. Entry criteria

- PAY-007 financial state decision is approved.
- WS04 transaction, invariant, and migration approach is available.
- WS02 provides worker deployment and secret injection foundation.
- WS03 defines high-risk financial permissions.
- WS09 correlation and telemetry conventions are approved or sufficiently stable.

#### J. Exit criteria

- Durable jobs have explicit claim, lease, retry, crash, replay, exhausted, and version behavior.
- Payment entitlement remains server/webhook authoritative under duplicates, ordering, and failures.
- Refund and credit workflows are provider-synchronized and concurrency-safe.
- Reconciliation detects and safely resolves controlled mismatches.
- Current tests, Stripe sandbox evidence, worker runtime evidence, dashboards, alerts, and runbooks are complete.

#### K. Risks and rollback considerations

- Creating a queue without transactional handoff from domain state.
- Retrying non-idempotent provider calls after unknown outcomes.
- Treating a local refund record as provider completion.
- Deploying incompatible worker and payload versions.
- Allowing manual repair without action-specific authorization and audit.

#### E. Database and migration implications

#### I. Entry criteria

- STO-006, STO-009, OPS-020, and retention decisions are assigned or approved.
- WS04 supports object-state schema changes.
- WS05 durable jobs are available for processing/reconciliation where required.
- R2 test/staging bucket access is available without exposing credentials.

#### J. Exit criteria

- Upload authority and file validation are enforced by server-side evidence, not client claims.
- Signed URL behavior and provider controls are verified.
- Object state, cleanup, deletion, and recovery follow approved lifecycle policy.
- Reconciliation detects and safely handles missing/orphan/divergent objects.
- Current tests, sandbox evidence, alerts, and runbooks are complete.

#### K. Risks and rollback considerations

- Deleting objects before retention and restore policy is approved.
- Breaking existing URLs or cache behavior during key/version changes.
- Using synchronous image processing that exceeds request budgets without a decision.
- Listing or logging object keys that reveal personal or private context.

Source-map and telemetry exposure must be deliberate. | WS02, WS08, WS09, WS10 | Frontend platform owner | Approved browser/performance policy plus build/browser evidence. |
| GOV-004 | Assign named production owners across core systems and provider accounts. | Assign named owners for authentication, authorization, payments, database, storage, workers, deployment, secrets, monitoring, backups, incident response, privacy operations, and provider accounts. | WS02, WS03, WS04, WS05, WS06, WS07, WS08, WS09, WS10 | Engineering leadership | Owner matrix plus provider-access records. |

|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Production build and public configuration | STATIC CONFIGURATION / DEPLOYMENT | Define Vite mode, targets, base, source maps, release identity, public variable allowlist, and artifact evidence. | FE-M01, FE-M02 | WS02 environment/release model and TST-017 decision | Wrong environment or sensitive build data reaches the browser. |
| API client and request lifecycle | CODE | Centralize token, correlation, timeout, cache, retry, cancellation, and credentials behavior. | FE-M03, FE-M04, FE-M11 | WS02 API/error contract | Stale responses overwrite state or destructive requests retry unsafely. |
| Identity-scoped state and routing | CODE / CURRENT TESTS | Clear and partition caches/state across login, logout, user switch, role change, suspension, deletion, and direct routes. | FE-M05, FE-M06, FE-M10 | WS03 account-state policy and API cache contract | One user sees another user’s cached state or stale admin UI. |
| Forms and error behavior | CODE / CURRENT TESTS | Provide accessible validation, unknown-field handling, destructive-action safety, offline/retry states, and error boundaries. | FE-M07, FE-M11 | Stable backend error contract | Users repeat destructive actions or cannot recover from failures. |
| Browser security and third-party policy | OWNER DECISION / STATIC CONFIGURATION | Control URLs, CSP/SRI position, third-party inventory, provider failure, source maps, and sensitive browser storage. | FE-M08 through FE-M10 | API/header decisions and provider inventory | Unsafe URL execution, data sharing, or secret/client-state exposure. |
| Accessibility, browser support, and performance | OWNER DECISION / CURRENT TESTS | Set WCAG target, browser matrix, budgets, measurement, and verification. | FE-M12, FE-M13 | Product decisions and production build | Launch lacks defined user experience and accessibility evidence. |

#### E. Database and migration implications

Direct schema changes are generally outside this workstream. Frontend changes must remain compatible with rolling API/schema changes from WS02/WS04. Any new client cache keys or persisted identifiers must not become authority or bypass server-side lifecycle and authorization rules.

| Playwright browser tests | Login/logout, user switch, role removal, suspension/deletion, direct refresh, unknown route, private cache, forms. | Open tab after privilege loss, browser back/history, offline/provider failure, destructive retry. | Cross-context identity changes where safe. | FE-M05 through FE-M12, TST-003, TST-004 | Production-build Playwright results with artifacts sanitized. |
| Accessibility tests | Semantic roles, keyboard/focus, error association, contrast/reflow/zoom, dynamic updates. | Modal focus loss, hidden error, keyboard trap, zoom overflow. | Not applicable. | FE-M07, FE-M12 | Automated report plus approved manual audit evidence. |
| Performance/browser tests | Approved browsers and viewports, budgets, asset/source-map exposure, third-party failure. | Slow provider, large bundle, cache miss, mobile layout, unsupported browser. | Concurrent navigation/request load where relevant. | FE-M01, FE-M09, FE-M13 | Production-build measurements against approved policy. |

#### H. Operational deliverables

| Deliverable | Purpose | Suggested owner role | Related controls | Approval or exercise evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Frontend build and public-variable policy | Define production build, public inputs, artifact identity, and source maps. | Frontend platform owner | FE-M01, FE-M02, FE-M13 | Security/release approval. |
| Identity-scoped state policy | Define what is cached/persisted and how it is cleared on identity/state changes. | Frontend/auth owner | FE-M05, FE-M10 | Security review and browser tests. |
| Third-party browser-code policy | Approve inventory, data sharing, CSP/SRI posture, monitoring, and failure behavior. | Frontend security/privacy owner | FE-M09 | Privacy/security approval. |

| Browser/performance policy | Define support matrix, budgets, source-map and telemetry posture. | Frontend platform/product owner | FE-M13 | Approved measurements and exceptions. |

#### I. Entry criteria

- WS02 API, cache, error, and deployment contracts are stable.
- WS03 identity/account-state and authorization decisions are available.
- FE-M09, FE-M12, FE-M13 decisions are assigned or approved.
- WS08 provides production-build unit/Playwright harnesses.

#### J. Exit criteria

- Production frontend artifact is attributable and contains only approved public configuration.
- Identity-scoped state does not leak across logout, switch, suspension, deletion, or role change.
- Routing, errors, forms, URLs, browser storage, and third-party failures behave as approved.
- Accessibility and performance/browser-support evidence meets approved targets or has governed exceptions.

#### K. Risks and rollback considerations

- Persisting user-scoped data under global keys.
- Masking backend authorization gaps with route guards.
- Introducing a global retry that repeats non-idempotent requests.
- Shipping source maps or public variables without policy review.
- Treating automated accessibility checks as a complete manual audit.

#### A. Objective

Transform current non-legacy tests and CI into a separated, deterministic, security-aware release gate covering critical workflows, concurrency, migrations, providers, artifacts, and provenance.

#### E. Database and migration implications

The test infrastructure requires isolated PostgreSQL resources, reproducible schema setup, deterministic cleanup, and production-like migration fixtures. Concurrency tests must use real independent sessions and barriers. Migration tests must preserve fixtures representing prior schemas and interruption states. Test data must remain synthetic and governed by WS10.

| Critical-domain suites | Auth, payment, upload, workers, admin, privacy, backup/restore, deployment. | All locked negative/failure scenarios. | Required deterministic races and replay. | TST-002, TST-005 through TST-009 | Current non-legacy suite inventory mapped to controls. |
| CI workflow tests | Required job graph, change detection, aggregate gate, artifact upload, protected secrets, provider test segregation. | Skipped job, forked PR, failed artifact, unavailable provider, canceled workflow. | Parallel jobs and retry reporting. | TST-013 through TST-017 | Workflow/static checker evidence and actual CI records collected later. |

#### I. Entry criteria

- WS01 approves test, flake, artifact, and release decisions.
- Each implementation workstream identifies required current tests and fixtures.
- Isolated PostgreSQL, browser, and provider-sandbox environments are defined.
- No legacy test is treated as current evidence.

#### J. Exit criteria

- Every unresolved control has current test or explicit non-test evidence mapped.
- Critical payments, uploads, authorization, concurrency, jobs, migration, privacy, and recovery scenarios are covered.
- CI gates the applicable suites and scans, and GitHub settings require the aggregate result.
- Artifacts and release evidence are attributable, retained, sanitized, and linked to deployment.

#### K. Risks and rollback considerations

- Creating a huge nondeterministic end-to-end suite instead of layered tests.
- Running provider tests with production resources or credentials.
- Using retries to hide flaky behavior.
- Adding gates before fixtures and isolation are stable, causing teams to bypass them.
- Counting workflow configuration as proof that it ran.

### WS09: Logging, metrics, correlation, dashboards, alerts, and capacity

#### A. Objective

Establish privacy-safe structured audit/logging, correlation, metrics, service objectives, dashboards, alerts, capacity models, and operational visibility for all critical flows.

#### B. Locked findings addressed

| Control ID | Priority | Final status | Locked gap | Why included |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| API-M15 | P0 | FAIL | Search over backend, .github, README, and config for request-id middleware, X-Request-ID, correlation IDs, route-template access logs, structured logging config, centralized redaction, and safe logging policy found no production access-log/correlation system. | It governs auditability, logging, correlation, metrics, dashboards, alerts, or capacity. |
| ADM-001 | P0 | PARTIAL | Separation is proven for several domain records, but access/application logs are not modeled as a durable separate audit store in the searched scope: backend/models, backend/services, backend/routes, backend/alembic/versions, .github/workflows, docs. Logger and script print boundaries remain ordinary logs, not controlled audit records. | It governs auditability, logging, correlation, metrics, dashboards, alerts, or capacity. |
| ADM-002 | P0 | PARTIAL | Privileged success auditing is broad but not universal. Sensitive reads are not audited. Authorization denials are mostly dependency-level 401/403 responses without an audit row, with only selected guarded business rejections recorded. Duplicate/replay outcomes generally return existing rows rather than recording replay attempts. Provider failures and security/access changes are selectively represented. Runtime log/audit completeness was not verified. | It governs auditability, logging, correlation, metrics, dashboards, alerts, or capacity. |
| ADM-003 | P0 | PARTIAL | Static search scope backend/models, backend/services, backend/routes, backend/alembic/versions did not show required request ID/correlation ID, environment/release, stable outcome/failure-code fields for every audit row, or actor role-context snapshots. Before/after snapshots are allowed in metadata when keys pass validation, so minimized selected-change storage is not guaranteed. | It governs auditability, logging, correlation, metrics, dashboards, alerts, or capacity. |
| ADM-004 | P0 | PARTIAL | No DB trigger, RLS, privilege separation, or append-only database enforcement was found in backend/models, backend/alembic/versions, or DB config. App-path creation is not equivalent to DB-level immutability or provider role protection. | It governs auditability, logging, correlation, metrics, dashboards, alerts, or capacity. |
| ADM-005 | P0 | PARTIAL | Atomicity is inconsistent around external providers and failure paths. Account deletion commits staged deletion before Firebase deletion and later writes final audit at backend/services/admin_user_delete_service.py:889-983 and 1091-1138; refund retry records and flushes an admin action before Stripe retry at backend/services/admin_money_refund_service.py:610-655; provider failures roll back selected DB work at backend/services/admin_money_refund_service.py:656-667, but there is no repository-wide fail-closed audit-write policy. Static search found no external audit forwarding sink using syslog, SIEM, audit webhook, Splunk, Datadog, OpenTelemetry, SQS/Kinesis/EventBridge, or similar terms under backend source/docs excluding tests and legacy tests. | It governs auditability, logging, correlation, metrics, dashboards, alerts, or capacity. |
| ADM-006 | P0 | PARTIAL | Redaction is present for admin action metadata, but not proven as a repository-wide logging boundary. Moderation exception logs include structured target variables and stack traces. Script output lacks explicit terminal/newline/formula protections, though current examples are operational scripts rather than public API exports. No runtime log-capture review was performed. | It governs auditability, logging, correlation, metrics, dashboards, alerts, or capacity. |
| OPS-008 | P0 | FAIL | No centralized structured frontend, API, worker, database, provider, or edge logs with release, environment, request, job, or event context, restricted access, redaction, loss detection, or policy-driven retention. Search scope is detailed in OPS-008 appendix. | It governs auditability, logging, correlation, metrics, dashboards, alerts, or capacity. |
| OPS-009 | P0 | FAIL | No operational metrics for API traffic/errors/latency, database pool/query/lock/storage, worker backlog, payment/webhook/refund/reconciliation outcomes, upload/storage divergence, auth failures, release health, backup success, or provider quotas. Search scope is detailed in OPS-009 appendix. | It governs auditability, logging, correlation, metrics, dashboards, alerts, or capacity. |

#### D. Repository implementation scope

| Component or area | Planned change category | Intended outcome | Related controls | Dependency | Risk if sequenced incorrectly |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Request and event correlation | CODE / OBSERVABILITY | Generate bounded request/job/payment correlation and stable error context. | API-M15, OPS-010 | Telemetry/privacy decision | Unbounded or sensitive labels create privacy and cost risks. |
| Administrative audit trail | DATABASE / CODE | Separate append-only audit records, coverage, structure, atomicity, redaction, and restricted access. | ADM-001 through ADM-006 | WS03 permissions and WS04 transactions | Consequential actions succeed without reliable audit or leak sensitive data. |
| Central logging | OBSERVABILITY / DEPLOYMENT | Aggregate frontend, API, worker, database, provider, and edge logs with release/environment context, access, redaction, loss detection, retention. | OPS-008 | Deployment/provider access and data classification | Incidents cannot be reconstructed or logs expose secrets/PII. |
| Metrics, dashboards, and alerts | OBSERVABILITY | Measure user, financial, data, worker, backup, provider, and release outcomes and deliver alerts. | OPS-009, OPS-011 | Stable workflows and SLO/threshold decisions | Failures remain invisible or alerts are noisy and ignored. |
| Service objectives and capacity | OWNER DECISION / OPERATIONAL PROCESS | Define indicators, objectives, capacity/cost model, quotas, and alert-before-limit evidence. | OPS-012, OPS-016 | GOV-006 and production topology | Scaling and launch decisions lack measurable bounds. |

#### E. Database and migration implications

Audit and telemetry schemas may require request/release/environment context, append-only permissions, and indexed correlation fields. Avoid turning high-cardinality telemetry into transactional application tables without a design decision. Audit writes tied to privileged mutations must preserve atomicity or an explicit durable handoff. Retention and archive behavior depend on WS10 policies.

#### F. Current-test plan

| Test layer | Required coverage | Failure cases | Concurrency or replay needs | Related controls | Completion evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Logging/redaction tests | Structured fields, route templates, request IDs, encoding, secret/PII/private-message exclusion. | Newline/JSON/HTML/spreadsheet injection, provider exception, malformed request. | Concurrent requests maintain separate context. | API-M15, ADM-003, ADM-006, OPS-008 | Current tests assert safe normalized records. |
| Audit integration tests | Success, sensitive read, denial, conflict, duplicate/replay, provider failure, atomicity, append-only permissions. | Audit write failure, rollback, retry, duplicate event, unauthorized unmask/export. | Concurrent privileged actions. | ADM-001 through ADM-007 | Current PostgreSQL tests and denied update/delete evidence. |
| Metrics/alert tests | Known user/financial/data/worker failure produces bounded metrics and an alert. | Telemetry backend unavailable, dropped logs, high-cardinality input, maintenance window. | Load and backlog thresholds using approved values. | OPS-009 through OPS-012, OPS-016 | Alert delivery and dashboard query evidence. |
| Capacity/load tests | API/DB/job/provider quota behavior and alert-before-limit. | Pool wait, backlog growth, quota denial, log ingestion pressure. | Controlled concurrent load. | GOV-006, OPS-016 | Synthetic load report tied to approved capacity model. |

#### H. Operational deliverables

| Deliverable | Purpose | Suggested owner role | Related controls | Approval or exercise evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Telemetry and correlation policy | Define allowed attributes, bounds, privacy review, request/job/payment correlation, tracing choice. | Observability/privacy owner | OPS-010 | Approved policy and sample review. |
| Audit event catalog and access policy | Define required actions/outcomes/fields, append-only model, sensitive-read/unmask/export rules. | Security/audit owner | ADM-001 through ADM-008 | Security/privacy approval and test coverage. |
| SLI/SLO and launch-threshold record | Define service and business indicators/objectives without invented values. | Operations/business owner | OPS-012 | Owner approval and dashboard mapping. |
| Capacity and cost model | Map API, DB, workers, providers, storage, logs, CI, and backups. | Platform/finance owner | OPS-016 | Load/provider evidence and review. |
| Dashboard and alert catalog | Map symptom-based alerts to user, financial, data, security, and recovery outcomes. | Reliability owner | OPS-009, OPS-011 | Delivery test and runbook linkage. |

#### I. Entry criteria

- Telemetry, SLO, capacity, and label decisions are assigned or approved.
- WS02 provides release/environment context.
- WS04/WS05/WS06 define durable events and states.
- WS10 defines data classification and retention boundaries.

#### J. Exit criteria

- Request, job, payment, and admin activity is correlated without sensitive/unbounded labels.
- Audit records are distinct, minimum-necessary, append-only in the normal path, and access-controlled.
- Central logs and metrics cover all critical components and detect data loss.
- Dashboards and alerts map to approved objectives and have delivery evidence.
- Capacity and cost evidence supports launch decisions.

#### K. Risks and rollback considerations

- Logging secrets, presigned URLs, private messages, or payment context while adding visibility.
- High-cardinality labels causing cost or denial-of-service.
- Alerting on implementation metrics instead of user/financial/data symptoms.
- Making privileged mutations unavailable because audit failure behavior is not designed.
- Collecting telemetry without retention and access controls.

### WS10: Incident response, runbooks, secrets, backups, restore, privacy, retention, and recovery exercises

#### A. Objective

Establish secret and control-plane governance, incident response, runbooks, provider outage handling, backup/PITR, restore, privacy operations, retention, and exercised recovery.

#### E. Database and migration implications

DB-011 and privacy/retention decisions may require phased lifecycle columns, anonymization/tombstone records, export tracking, durable deletion retry, and restore-time replay mechanisms. Backup and restore testing must use isolated environments and preserve referential, identity, Stripe/R2, job, and deletion semantics. Destructive data changes require legal approval and forward-compatible migrations.

| Secret/access process tests | Rotation, revocation, safe overlap, lost factor, offboarding, emergency access. | Expired credential, compromised key, unavailable owner, accidental production access. | Not applicable except overlapping rotation. | OPS-005 through OPS-007 | Sanitized exercise and access-review records. |
| Incident/runbook exercises | API/DB/provider/worker/payment/storage/secret/certificate/backup scenarios. | Alert failure, communication gap, unsafe repair, missing reconciliation. | Parallel symptoms and provider outage where useful. | OPS-013 through OPS-015, OPS-021 | Tabletop and technical exercise reports with actions. |
| Backup/restore tests | PITR, decryption, integrity, application startup, identity, Stripe/R2 refs, jobs, deletion tombstones. | Missing credential, corrupt backup, incompatible version, stale provider reference. | Restore while new writes are isolated; replay deletion. | OPS-017 through OPS-021, OPS-023 | Isolated restore report and measured RPO/RTO evidence. |

| Isolated restore | Dedicated recovery environment | Restore report covering integrity, startup, mappings, provider refs, jobs, and deletion replay. | OPS-019, OPS-023 | Backup/PITR and stable application version. |
| Incident and recovery exercises | Tabletop and controlled technical environments | Scenario, participants, timeline, observations, decisions, actions, owners, retest. | OPS-013 through OPS-015, OPS-021 | Monitoring, runbooks, and recovery access. |
| Privacy and retention enforcement | Staging plus provider settings | Synthetic request records, retention jobs/settings, export/deletion outcomes, backup handling. | GOV-003, DB-011, OPS-022 through OPS-024 | Approved schedules and legal review. |

#### H. Operational deliverables

| Deliverable | Purpose | Suggested owner role | Related controls | Approval or exercise evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Data classification and handling policy | Drive access, display, logging, cache, retention, backup, export, and deletion. | Privacy/security owner | GOV-003 | Legal/security approval. |
| Data lifecycle and retention schedule | Choose lifecycle for every table/data category and provider copy. | Privacy/legal/data owner | DB-011, OPS-022 | Approved schedule and enforcement review. |
| Secret inventory and lifecycle procedures | Record owner, scope, storage, rotation, revocation, emergency response, and short-lived identity decision. | Security/platform owner | OPS-006, OPS-007 | Access review and rotation exercise. |
| Control-plane access and offboarding register | Document users, roles, MFA, recovery owner, least privilege, and offboarding. | Security/operations owner | OPS-005, OPS-025 | Provider review. |
| Incident response plan | Define severity, roles, containment, evidence, recovery, reconciliation, communication, review, and actions. | Incident commander/security owner | OPS-013, OPS-014 | Tabletop exercise. |
| Operational runbooks | Provide safe procedures for all listed outage and recovery scenarios. | Service owners coordinated by operations | OPS-015 | Technical exercises. |
| RPO/RTO and backup record | Approve objectives and provider backup/PITR design. | Business/operations/database owner | OPS-017, OPS-018 | Provider evidence and restore result. |
| Restore and recovery reports | Prove isolated restore, deletion replay, provider references, and recovery exercises. | Recovery owner | OPS-019 through OPS-021, OPS-023 | Approved exercise actions and retest. |
| Privacy request procedures | Define access, correction, export, deletion, retry, backup/log/R2 handling, and legal review. | Privacy operations owner | OPS-023, OPS-024 | Synthetic end-to-end request evidence. |

#### I. Entry criteria

- WS01 assigns operations, security, privacy, legal, provider, and recovery owners.
- Required retention, RPO/RTO, R2 recovery, and exercise decisions are approved before destructive or exercise work.
- WS02-WS06 designs are stable enough to document and restore.
- WS09 logs, metrics, dashboards, and alerts are available.
- Provider access is available through safe evidence procedures.

#### J. Exit criteria

- Secrets and control-plane access are inventoried, least-privilege, reviewed, and recoverable.
- Incident response and runbooks are approved and exercised.
- Backup/PITR settings are evidenced and an isolated restore validates application, identity, provider references, jobs, and deletion replay.
- Retention and privacy procedures are approved and verified with synthetic data.
- Recovery actions are tracked to closure and retested.

#### K. Risks and rollback considerations

- Performing destructive retention or deletion work before legal/product decisions.
- Assuming provider backup enablement equals restore readiness.
- Storing sensitive provider screenshots or exports as audit evidence.
- Writing runbooks that do not match the final deployed architecture.
- Restoring old data without replaying deletion/anonymization requirements.

Secret-handling restriction: never collect raw tokens, private keys, passwords, webhook secrets, recovery codes, full connection strings, payment card data, full unredacted user exports, database dumps, presigned URLs, or sensitive incident evidence. Evidence must be minimized, access-controlled, and redacted before preservation.

## 11. Runtime-verification program

| Verification program | Scenarios | Related controls | Required environment | Data strategy | Pass evidence | Failure handling |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| HTTP edge and transport | Trusted/untrusted proxy, direct origin, host, HTTPS redirects, HSTS, CORS allow/deny, headers, cache classes. | API-M04 through API-M08, API-M16, API-M19, OPS-025 | Staging behind production-equivalent edge. | Synthetic requests and public/non-sensitive resources. | Header/redirect traces and denial results tied to release. | Record defect by control ID; stop sign-off for unsafe transport or cache behavior. |
| Limits, timeouts, and abuse controls | Bodies, multipart, headers, URLs, pagination, bulk, timeout/cancellation, rate scopes, multi-instance behavior. | API-M09 through API-M11, DB-002, GOV-006, OPS-016 | Staging with safe synthetic load. | Generated payloads and synthetic users/resources. | Boundary responses, resource release, Retry-After behavior, pool/backlog metrics. | Abort unsafe load, preserve sanitized observations, and revise decisions or implementation. |
| Errors, correlation, and logs | Validation, malformed JSON, provider/DB failures, timeout, unexpected exception, request/job/payment correlation and redaction. | API-M12, API-M15, ADM-006, OPS-008, OPS-010 | Staging with sanitized log access. | Synthetic errors containing injection and secret-like markers. | Stable JSON errors and redacted correlated records. | Stop if credentials, private messages, payment data, or unnecessary PII appear. |
| Identity and account state | Token revocation, disablement, email verification, persistence, App Check decision, recent auth, recovery, suspension, deletion, role removal. | IAM-001, IAM-003, IAM-004, IAM-007 through IAM-011, IAM-016 through IAM-018 | Firebase test project and staging backend/frontend. | Synthetic accounts and controlled claims. | Transition matrix and denial timing without tokens. | Revoke test credentials and block dependent work if authority conflicts remain. |
| Authorization substitution and stale state | Cross-user IDs, nested resources, workflow states, fields, list/search, admin actions, unmask/export, stale role. | IAM-012 through IAM-017, ADM-007, ADM-013, ADM-015, TST-005 | Staging API with synthetic role/resource matrix. | Synthetic users, games, bookings, messages, payments, moderation records. | Complete expected 403/404 and data-minimization matrix. | Treat any unauthorized access or sensitive response as a blocking defect. |
| Transactions and concurrency | Capacity, active records, webhooks, credits/refunds, admin actions, job claims, deadlocks, timeouts, unknown commits. | DB-004 through DB-009, PAY-002, PAY-010, JOB-M03, TST-008 | Isolated PostgreSQL integration/staging environment. | Synthetic rows and deterministic barriers. | Repeatable winner/loser, retry, rollback, and final-state evidence. | Stop on nondeterminism; capture transaction state and redesign before broader load. |
| Stripe lifecycle and reconciliation | Checkout outcomes, callback, webhook signature/order/replay/delay, refund/credit, dispute/events, timeout/unknown, mismatch repair. | PAY-001 through PAY-013, TST-006 | Stripe test mode and staging backend/worker. | Stripe test objects and synthetic app users. | Redacted event/state timeline and reconciliation outcome. | Do not retry unknown provider effects blindly; route to durable reconciliation. |
| R2 upload and divergence | Signed URL method/expiry, CORS, file validation, confirmation, processing, missing/orphan object, cleanup, deletion, recovery. | STO-001 through STO-009, TST-007 | R2 test bucket and staging API/worker. | Synthetic image corpus and controlled divergent metadata. | Redacted object-state, rejection, reconciliation, and cleanup evidence. | Quarantine unsafe files and prevent destructive cleanup until classification is known. |
| Durable jobs | Claim/lease, heartbeat, duplicate worker, crash before/after side effect, retry, replay, exhaustion, shutdown, rolling version, monitoring. | JOB-M01 through JOB-M08, ADM-011, ADM-016 | Staging worker and durable queue/database. | Synthetic jobs with controlled failure injection. | Job/attempt state, replay, backlog, alerts, and repair evidence. | Pause affected handlers on duplicate side effects or unrecoverable state. |
| Frontend session and browser behavior | Production build, logout/switch, suspension/deletion, stale admin, private cache/history, routes, errors, accessibility, performance, provider failure. | FE-M01 through FE-M13, TST-003, TST-004 | Staging production build and approved browser matrix. | Synthetic accounts/content and sanitized artifacts. | Browser, accessibility, bundle, and performance reports. | Block release on cross-user cache leakage, unsafe URL behavior, or unresolved critical accessibility/security failures. |
| Deployment and migration compatibility | Startup/readiness/shutdown, rolling old/new API/worker/webhook/job/schema, migration timing/interruption/forward-fix. | API-M01, API-M03, API-M17, DB-016 through DB-018, OPS-001, OPS-004, TST-009 | Staging deployment and production-like synthetic database. | Versioned synthetic dataset and release artifacts. | Health, lifecycle, compatibility, lock, and recovery report. | Stop rollout on incompatible versions, blocking migration, or failed readiness. |
| Backup, restore, privacy, and exercises | Backup/PITR, decryption, integrity, app startup, identity/provider refs, jobs, deletion replay, privacy requests, incident and recovery scenarios. | GOV-003, DB-011, OPS-013 through OPS-024 | Isolated recovery environment and tabletop/technical exercise settings. | Synthetic personal/financial/object records with deletion tombstones. | Restore and exercise reports with RPO/RTO observations and tracked actions. | Do not expose restored synthetic environment; resolve gaps and retest before sign-off. |

## 12. Testing and CI transformation plan

| Capability | Current locked gap | Planned result | Controls | Dependency | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Test taxonomy | Suites are incompletely separated and named. | Document and enforce unit, service, API/PostgreSQL, deterministic concurrency, mocked browser, full-stack browser, provider sandbox, migration, security, smoke, and exercise categories. | TST-001, TST-004, TST-010 | GOV-002 environment matrix and test ownership. | Suite inventory, commands, configs, fixtures, and control mapping. |
| Current backend suites | Coverage is concentrated in Browse Games, My Games, shared auth, and selected constraints. | Preserve current non-legacy useful suites while expanding critical domain coverage. | TST-002, TST-005 | Stable domain interfaces. | Current test inventory and execution records later. |
| Payment tests | No current direct critical payment suite. | Cover checkout, webhook, refund, credit, dispute/event, failure, ordering, and reconciliation states. | TST-006, PAY-001 through PAY-013 | WS05 state/job design and Stripe sandbox. | Current unit/integration/sandbox reports. |
| Upload/storage tests | No current direct critical upload/R2 suite. | Cover authorization, file validation, signed URLs, confirmation, processing, divergence, cleanup, and recovery. | TST-007, STO-001 through STO-009 | WS06 policy and R2 test bucket. | Current unit/integration/sandbox reports. |
| Deterministic concurrency | No barrier-coordinated current race tests. | Use independent sessions/tasks, synchronization barriers, winner/loser assertions, cleanup, and repeatability. | TST-008, DB-007, DB-008, PAY-010, JOB-M03 | WS04 PostgreSQL harness and stable invariants. | Repeatable concurrency results. |
| Migration assurance | CI covers empty upgrade intent only. | Add prior-schema, drift, multi-head, interruption, data preservation, old/new overlap, and forward-fix coverage. | TST-009, DB-016 through DB-018 | Versioned fixtures and release model. | Migration artifacts and rehearsal reports. |
| Frontend unit tests | Selected unit coverage exists but not broad state/security coverage. | Cover API client, identity-scoped state, routes, errors, URL validation, forms, cache, cleanup, and accessibility helpers. | TST-002, TST-003, FE-M03 through FE-M13 | WS07 contracts stable. | Current unit suite reports. |
| Playwright/full-stack browser | One landing spec and limited configuration. | Run production build with deterministic synthetic users/data, sanitized artifacts, identity transitions, guards, routes, accessibility, and critical workflows. | TST-003, TST-004, FE-M05, FE-M06, FE-M10, FE-M12, FE-M13 | Staging/full-stack environment. | Browser results and sanitized trace/screenshot/video policy. |
| Provider sandbox separation | Stripe, Firebase, and R2 boundaries are incomplete. | Separate provider suites, credentials, resources, triggers, cleanup, and reporting from mocked/full-stack tests. | TST-004, TST-006, TST-007, TST-010 | Provider projects and secret process. | Environment matrix and provider suite reports. |
| CI gating sequence | Many required gates are absent. | Gate lint/format/type, unit, backend/PostgreSQL, concurrency, migration, browser, security scans, build artifacts, and approved provider suites in dependency order. | TST-013 | Stable commands and isolation. | Workflow graph and later CI run evidence. |
| Tool/dependency reproducibility | Tool versions and backend lock integrity are incomplete. | Pin package managers/runtime tools, verify clean installs, lock dependency resolution, and detect mismatch. | TST-012 | Release/tool decisions. | Version and lock validation evidence. |
| Security and supply-chain scans | No complete secret, dependency, source, bundle, container/OS, SBOM, suppression, or revocation process. | Add approved scanners, ownership/expiry for suppressions, history response, SBOM/provenance linkage, and review gates. | TST-015, TST-016, TST-017 | Artifact and secret policies. | Scan reports, suppression register, SBOM/provenance evidence. |
| Frontend production-build validation | CI build intent does not prove production artifact safety. | Validate production mode, public variable allowlist, source maps, release ID, bundle, and deployed artifact identity. | FE-M01, FE-M02, TST-013, TST-017 | WS02/WS07 build design. | Artifact scan and deployment linkage. |
| Backend deployable artifact validation | No trusted backend image or platform-native artifact strategy. | Build and inspect a versioned deployable artifact or approved platform-native equivalent with exclusion and scan evidence. | OPS-002, OPS-003, TST-013, TST-016, TST-017 | WS02 runtime strategy decision. | Artifact identity, contents, scan, and runtime evidence. |
| Branch protection and required checks | Workflow YAML cannot prove enforcement. | Require reviewed aggregate checks, reviews, controlled bypass, workflow ownership, and protected environments as approved. | TST-014, TST-015 | Final CI job names and GitHub access. | Redacted ruleset evidence. |
| Flake and artifact handling | No owner/expiry, retry, sanitization, or retention policy. | Approve diagnostic retries, quarantine rules, owner/expiry, root-cause tracking, sanitized artifacts, and retention. | TST-011 | Quality/release decision. | Policy plus CI artifact evidence. |
| Release provenance | No immutable artifact/source/test/deployment linkage. | Approve and collect release identity, source and lock linkage, test approvals, SBOM/provenance/signing posture, and deployment record. | TST-017 | Artifact strategy and CI gates. | Release evidence bundle. |

## 13. Operational and governance deliverable register

| Deliverable | Controls | Required contents | Suggested owner role | Approval needed | Validation or exercise |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Production architecture and trust boundaries | GOV-001 | Components, providers, data flows, regions, public/private boundaries, entry points, dependencies, and single points of failure. | Platform architecture owner | Engineering and security | Review against provider topology and recovery dependencies. |
| Environment matrix | GOV-002 | Local, automated test, browser test, provider sandbox, staging, production, projects, modes, buckets, DBs, domains, webhooks, secrets, logs, and CI credentials. | Platform owner | Security and release | Provider cross-check and isolation verification. |
| Data classification and handling policy | GOV-003 | Public, internal, personal, private-message, financial, moderation, audit, authentication, and secret handling across access, display, logs, cache, retention, backup, export, and deletion. | Privacy/security owner | Legal, product, security | Privacy workflow and telemetry review. |
| Production ownership register | GOV-004 | Named accountable and backup owners for systems, providers, security, release, monitoring, backup, incident, and privacy functions. | Engineering leadership | Leadership | Provider access and escalation validation. |
| Production-readiness audit process | GOV-005 | Cadence, normalized status, evidence, source linkage, approval, versioning, exceptions, preserved results, and reassessment triggers. | Release governance owner | Engineering/security leadership | Rehearsed audit cycle. |
| Risk and exception register | GOV-007 | Owner, rationale, scope, compensating controls, expiry/review date, verification, and closure. | Security/risk owner | Business and security | Expired-exception and reassessment review. |
| Limits and threshold decision record | GOV-006, DB-002, OPS-010, OPS-012, OPS-016 | Request, timeout, rate, pool, worker, retry, retention, RPO/RTO, telemetry, SLO, alert, capacity, and cost rationale and validation method. | Engineering/operations/business owners | Affected owners | Boundary, load, dashboard, and exercise evidence. |
| Secret inventory | IAM-011, OPS-006, OPS-007 | Owner, scope, environment, dependent systems, storage, access, rotation, revocation, emergency response, overlap, and identity model. | Security/platform owner | Security | Rotation/revocation exercise. |
| Access review and offboarding register | GOV-004, OPS-005, OPS-025 | Provider/control-plane users, roles, MFA, least privilege, recovery owner, review, and offboarding. | Security operations owner | Provider owners | Redacted provider cross-check. |
| API and browser policy decisions | API-M08, API-M18, FE-M09, FE-M12, FE-M13 | Header ownership, docs/versioning, third-party code, WCAG target, browser support, performance, source maps, and telemetry posture. | API/frontend/product/security owners | Security, product, accessibility | Runtime/browser verification. |
| Identity and high-risk action policy | IAM-003, IAM-006 through IAM-010, IAM-017 | Persistence, authority, verified email, recent auth/MFA, App Check, recovery, and privileged action controls. | Auth/security owner | Product, privacy, operations | Provider settings and account-state tests. |
| Audit and moderation policy | ADM-008, ADM-009, ADM-014 | Audit lifecycle, taxonomy/versioning, review, enforcement notices, suppression, appeals, and legal handling. | Security/trust-and-safety/legal owners | Legal, privacy, product | Review, access, notice, and retention evidence. |
| Financial state and reconciliation policy | PAY-007, PAY-012, PAY-013 | Canonical states, authority, provider events, unknown outcomes, disputes, repair, reconciliation, and permissions. | Payments/finance owner | Finance, security, operations | Stripe sandbox and reconciliation exercise. |
| Storage lifecycle and recovery policy | STO-006, STO-009, OPS-020 | Image processing, object classes, pending/replacement/deletion lifecycle, retention, recovery, and monitoring. | Storage/security/privacy owner | Security, privacy, operations | R2 evidence and divergence/recovery exercise. |
| Test, flake, and release evidence policy | TST-011, TST-017 | Suite risk, retries, quarantine, owner/expiry, artifacts, retention, artifact identity, SBOM/provenance/signing, approvals, and deployment linkage. | Quality/release owner | Security and engineering | CI and release evidence bundle. |
| SLI/SLO and alert record | OPS-010, OPS-011, OPS-012 | Indicators, objectives, labels, correlation, thresholds, alerts, routing, maintenance suppression, and error-budget posture. | Reliability/business owner | Business and service owners | Dashboard mapping and alert delivery test. |
| Capacity and cost model | OPS-016 | API, DB, worker, provider, R2, logs, CI, backups, quotas, load, budget, and alert-before-limit evidence. | Platform/finance owner | Business and service owners | Synthetic load and provider-plan review. |
| Incident response plan | OPS-013, OPS-014 | Severity, roles, containment, evidence, recovery, reconciliation, communication, provider status, post-incident review, owners, and actions. | Incident response owner | Security, operations, communications, service owners | Tabletop exercise. |
| Operational runbooks | OPS-015 | API/DB, connection exhaustion, release/migration, jobs/dead letters, Stripe mismatch, R2, Firebase, secret compromise, certificate, backup, and restore procedures. | Operations with service owners | Operations/security | Technical exercises. |
| Retention schedule | DB-011, OPS-022 | Purpose, system, data class, active/archived/backup/test retention, deletion/anonymization, legal hold, and owner. | Privacy/legal/data owner | Legal and business | Runtime/provider enforcement review. |
| Privacy request procedures | OPS-023, OPS-024 | Access, correction, export, deletion, durable retry, R2, logs, backups, restore reapplication, production data/dump/export controls. | Privacy operations owner | Legal/security | Synthetic end-to-end request test. |
| Backup and RPO/RTO records | OPS-017, OPS-018 | Provider settings, encryption, access, monitoring, retention, PITR, RPO/RTO, dependencies, credentials, and owner. | Database/recovery owner | Business, security, privacy | Provider review and restore test. |
| Restore report | OPS-019, OPS-023 | Decryption, integrity, startup, identity, Stripe/R2 references, jobs, deletion tombstones, duration, gaps, and actions. | Recovery owner | Database, security, privacy, service owners | Isolated restore approval. |
| Recovery exercise records | OPS-021 | Scenario, scope, participants, expected/actual results, timeline, gaps, actions, owners, due state, and retest. | Incident/recovery owner | Leadership and affected owners | Tabletop and technical exercise completion. |
| Provider evidence register | OPS-025 | Provider, setting/evidence, owner, collection date, environment, redaction, access, linked controls, review, and expiry/recheck. | Operations governance owner | Provider owners and security | Completeness and secret-handling review. |
