### 14.3 Coverage validation

| Validation item | Result |
|---|---|
| Total controls in the audit | 163 |
| Total unresolved controls | 163 |
| Total P0 unresolved controls | 117 |
| Total controls mapped to a primary remediation workstream | 163 |
| Unresolved controls not mapped | 0 |
| Duplicate primary mappings | 0 |
| Controls with one or more secondary workstreams | 156 |
| Final coverage result | PASS |

Every unresolved control has exactly one primary workstream. Secondary mappings do not change ownership or audit status; they identify evidence and dependency relationships.

## 15. Implementation-pass strategy

| Pass type | Maximum scope | Required input | Required output | Validation before merge | Stop condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Decision and governance pass | One coherent decision package or governance artifact with its linked control IDs. | Locked audit rows, remediation-plan section, participants, existing repository/provider context. | Approved decision or governance document only; no unrelated code. | Required participants review; downstream controls and evidence gates updated. | Stop when the decision artifact is complete or when unresolved authority prevents a valid choice. |
| Repository inspection pass | One narrow subsystem and exact audit-control set. | Current working tree, locked findings, intended workstream entry criteria. | Read-only current-state inventory and proposed file-level scope. | Confirm no stale audit assumption and no unrelated files are needed. | Stop before editing if scope conflicts with locked findings or requires an unmade decision. |
| Schema and migration pass | One compatible schema capability or migration dependency unit. | Approved design, current models/migrations, data compatibility rules, control IDs. | Models, migration, migration tests, compatibility and forward-fix notes. | PostgreSQL tests, graph/drift checks, production-like rehearsal plan. | Stop on destructive or blocking behavior not covered by approved migration strategy. |
| Domain implementation pass | One end-to-end invariant or workflow slice, not an entire workstream. | Approved decisions, inspected files, schema foundation, test plan, exact controls. | Code plus current unit/service/API tests and updated operational/interface docs. | Targeted tests, static checks, schema compatibility, no unrelated cleanup. | Stop when additional controls or architectural decisions are discovered outside scope. |
| Deterministic concurrency/failure pass | One race, replay, crash, timeout, or unknown-outcome family. | Stable workflow implementation, PostgreSQL/provider test doubles or sandbox, exact expected states. | Barrier/failure-injection tests and any narrowly required correction. | Repeatable results, winner/loser assertions, cleanup, final-state verification. | Stop on nondeterminism or ambiguous source of truth. |
| Frontend/browser pass | One identity/state/route/security/accessibility/performance slice. | Stable API/auth contract, production build config, synthetic browser fixtures, control IDs. | Frontend change, unit tests, Playwright coverage, sanitized artifact policy. | Production build, unit/browser tests, accessibility/security checks as applicable. | Stop if backend authorization or API contract must change outside scope. |
| CI/supply-chain pass | One gate family or artifact/provenance capability. | Stable commands, test taxonomy, secret boundary, branch-rule plan, control IDs. | Workflow/checker/config change and self-tests; no deployment unless explicitly scoped. | Workflow validation, least permissions, fork/secret analysis, required-check naming. | Stop if a gate would be bypassable or uses production credentials. |
| Provider-evidence pass | One provider/control plane and related controls. | Approved evidence checklist, named provider owner, safe redaction/storage process. | Redacted evidence package and unresolved discrepancy log; no secrets. | Two-person review where sensitive, environment and date attribution, control linkage. | Stop immediately if evidence contains secrets, personal data, or unapproved access. |
| Runtime-verification pass | One scenario family in a production-equivalent environment. | Merged implementation, current tests, provider config, synthetic data, rollback/abort criteria. | Sanitized observations, pass/fail by control, defects and follow-up dependencies. | Environment attribution, reproducibility, no production personal data, operational monitoring. | Stop on unsafe behavior, unexpected production impact, or missing rollback path. |
| Recovery/exercise pass | One tabletop, restore, or technical recovery scenario. | Approved runbook/objectives, isolated environment, backups/access, observers, control IDs. | Exercise report, measured observations, gaps, owners, actions, and retest requirement. | Integrity, startup, mappings, reconciliation, privacy/deletion replay, communication. | Stop if isolation, credentials, data safety, or recovery prerequisites are not proven. |

Every later Codex implementation prompt must name the exact audit control IDs, workstream, entry criteria, allowed files or subsystem, required tests, migration implications, prohibited scope expansion, and stop condition. Future prompts are intentionally not generated in this planning pass.

## 16. Final readiness closure sequence

1. **Repository changes:** Complete reviewed code, database, migration, static configuration, and operational-document changes in controlled passes.
2. **Current tests:** Add and execute the applicable current non-legacy unit, integration, PostgreSQL, concurrency, browser, provider, migration, and failure tests.
3. **CI/static verification:** Enforce reproducible tools, required checks, scans, build validation, artifacts, and supply-chain gates.
4. **Provider evidence:** Collect redacted control-plane settings, access, mode/environment, security, backup, and repository-protection evidence.
5. **Staging/runtime verification:** Verify edge, identity, authorization, transaction, payment, storage, job, browser, observability, and deployment behavior.
6. **Migration and concurrency rehearsal:** Exercise production-like schema upgrades, old/new compatibility, lock behavior, races, retries, and forward-fix paths.
7. **Backup and restore validation:** Prove backup/PITR access, isolated restore, integrity, startup, provider references, jobs, and deletion/anonymization replay.
8. **Incident and recovery exercises:** Execute approved runbooks, alert delivery, provider-outage, secret, worker, payment, storage, domain, and control-plane scenarios.
9. **Final control reassessment:** Reassess all 163 controls using current repository, runtime, provider, operational, and exercise evidence without carrying forward assumptions.
10. **Production-readiness decision:** Sign-off may occur only after every applicable P0 control is closed or formally resolved through an approved, time-bound exception and all required P1/P2 launch conditions are addressed.

Sign-off cannot occur earlier because implementation, tests, provider settings, runtime behavior, migration safety, and recovery evidence validate different failure boundaries. Passing one gate does not substitute for the others.

## 17. Risks, assumptions, and non-goals

- This final version was mechanically reconciled against the locked 163-control manifest and substantively reviewed for dependency order, owner-decision integrity, and audit-ready exit gates.
- This plan depends on the six locked audit reports and does not alter their findings.
- No runtime execution, CI execution, provider verification, deployment, backup, restore, or exercise occurred during planning.
- No timeout, limit, pool, retry, RPO, RTO, retention, SLO, alert, capacity, accessibility, browser, or performance value was invented.
- No unresolved owner decision was selected.
- Complexity bands are sequencing aids, not effort or calendar estimates.
- No code, test, migration, configuration, provider setting, or repository state was changed.
- Completion of this document does not close any audit control.
- Actual implementation may reveal current-tree facts that require a controlled scope review, but it must not silently change a locked audit result.
- Provider and operational evidence must remain redacted, minimized, access-controlled, and attributable to an environment and review date.

## 18. Completion statement

> Pickup Lane remediation planning completed from finalized Audit Parts 1 through 6. The plan preserves all locked findings, maps every unresolved control to phased remediation and evidence work, and performs no implementation. Repository changes must occur later through controlled implementation passes.
